docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex.md:6968:docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex.md:458:| 1 | **4.9.A** (W5-133, shipped) | `ReferenceMode` + `Locale {EnUs, De, Fr}` in `ql-types::eval_context`; `LocaleData` + `locale_data()` in `ql-formula-syntax::locale`; `Workbook::reference_mode` / `locale` accessors in `ql-storage`. Layering refined during implementation — enums in `ql-types`, separator data in `ql-formula-syntax`, no new dep-graph edge. |
docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex.md:6969:docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex.md:463:| 3 | **4.9.C parser** (W5-139, shipped) + **4.9.C binder** (W5-140, shipped) | **Parser (W5-139):** `Token::R1C1Ref` → intermediate `Expr::R1C1Ref { sheet, row_axis, col_axis }` (new variant) + `RangeRef::R1C1Cells` for ranges. Per-axis mixed-relativity check rejects with `ParseError::R1C1MixedRelativity`. Refined the design wording "Expr::CellRef with intermediate markers" to a new variant — keeps the 50+ `CellAddr` construction sites untouched. **Binder (W5-140):** `bind_with_context_v2` lowers `Expr::R1C1Ref` → `ExprPlan::CellRef { sheet, row, col, abs_col, abs_row }` using `BindSite::at_cell` for relative axes. Absolute axes drop straight in as `n - 1` (0-indexed). Relative axes resolve against `site.cell` (None → `BindError::R1C1RequiresAnchor`). Out-of-grid resolution → `BindError::R1C1OutOfBounds { resolved_row, resolved_col }`. The `abs_col` / `abs_row` flags on the emitted `ExprPlan::CellRef` are derived per-axis from the `AxisSpec` variant — preserves the abs/rel distinction the printer needs at 4.9.D. **Range lowering deferred** — `RangeRef::R1C1Cells` still surfaces via the existing `Expr::RangeRef(_)` `UnsupportedVariant` path (same as absolute `RangeRef::Cells`); range binding is handled by the existing aggregate-function dispatch and will fold R1C1Cells when that wave touches it. Calcgraph + printer `unreachable!()` stubs unchanged: storage canon (§ 4.4) lowers R1C1 → A1 text before fingerprint / stripe registration. |
docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex.md:6970:docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex.md:467:| 7 | **4.9.G** (W5-143, shipped, parser+printer-only) | `Token::At` (standalone `@`) + `Expr::ImplicitIntersection(Box<Expr>)` AST variant. Parser treats `@` as a prefix unary at bp 70 — looser than range `:` (so `@A:A` parses as `@(A:A)`, the Excel canon), tighter than every binary operator. Sheet-prefix interaction: `apply_sheet_to_term` gets a new arm that recurses INTO `ImplicitIntersection` to stamp the sheet onto the inner ref (so `Sheet1!@A1` and `@Sheet1!A1` produce identical ASTs). Walker touchpoints closed per Sonnet H-1: arms added to `rewrite_sheet_name_in_expr`, `rewrite_table_ref`, `rewrite_column_ref`, `print_expr_ctx`, `plan::variant_kind`, and `ql-calcgraph::fingerprint::hash_expr` (tag byte 13). **Binder stub** returns `BindError::UnsupportedVariant` pointing to 4.9.H (mirrors the 4.9.C pre-binder pattern). The in-bracket `@` inside `Sales[@Col]` stays inside `Token::StructuredRef.bracket_content` per OOXML escape rules — never reaches the new `Token::At` arm. Two prior tests assuming `@foo` was a lex error were updated to use backtick instead. |
docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex.md:6971:docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex.md:472:| 12 | **4.9.L** (W5-148, shipped) | Round-trip + edge case verification suite. +12 end-to-end property tests in `workbook_runtime::tests`: mixed-relativity range rejection at set_formula, `Sales[@Col]` vs `@Sales[Qty]` semantic equivalence, `@A:A` / `@1:1` whole-range narrowing at anchor, `Sheet1!@A1` cross-sheet, R1C1+EN round-trip through op-log replay (set_reference_mode + set_formula both replayed), DE+EN round-trip through replay, `@` preservation through replay, reference_mode/locale changes leave stored text canonical, `@scalar` idempotency, `@SUM(scalar args)` no-op. Note: rule 7 (function-returns-array → top-left) deferred — array-returning functions in scalar context still return `#CALC!` per existing 4.7.G limit; closing megaudit (4.9.O) decides whether to bundle that with cell-boundary spill rewiring or defer to a Phase 4.10 polish item. |
docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex.md:6972:docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex.md:473:| 13 | **4.9.M** (W5-149, shipped) | Table-driven coverage matrix `(mode × locale × @-presence)` = 30 cells. Each cell specifies a `(mode, locale, input)` triple and the expected canonical (A1+EnUs) output; the driver runs `set_formula(0, 0, 0, input)` and asserts the stored text matches. Modes: A1, R1C1. Locales: EnUs, De, Fr. `@`-presence: none, prefix-cell, prefix-function, inside-arg, prefix-rel-ref. 3 test fns: full-matrix driver, no-duplicate-cells invariant, per-axis dimension counts (pins 2/3/30 so future cell-adds trip the axis sanity test until the doc is updated). Confirms the design § 4.4 canon: stored text is mode + locale invariant; `@` is preserved verbatim. |
docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex.md:6973:docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex.md:490:| Q8 `Sheet1!@A1` | First-class in 4.9.G/H grammar production (NOT edge-case test). Codex + Sonnet agree. |

Locales: EnUs, De, Fr. `@`-presence: none, prefix-cell, prefix-function, inside-arg, prefix-rel-ref. 3 test fns: full-matrix driver, no-duplicate-cells invariant, per-axis dimension counts (pins 2/3/30 so future cell-adds trip the axis sanity test until the doc is updated). Confirms the design § 4.4 canon: stored text is mode + locale invariant; `@` is preserved verbatim. |

# Phase 4.9 closing megaudit (R1C1 + locales + implicit intersection)

You are reviewing the cumulative Phase 4.9 arc. The arc is feature-
complete pending this audit. Your job is to find integration-level
issues that single-phase audits missed — the kind that only surface
when multiple sub-phases interact.

This pattern caught HIGH BLOCKERS at the prior 4.7.O and 4.8.O
megaudits. Treat it as load-bearing — go deeper than "each sub-
phase reviewed individually."

## Context

- Branch: `feat/quantbook-engine`. HEAD `72f39a84446`.
- Design doc: `docs/architecture/2026-05-15-r1c1-locales-implicit-intersection.md`.
- Pre-arc HEAD: `27076f9925b` (W5-137 mid-arc audit closure, before
  the 4.9.B.4 → 4.9.M wave).
- 2611 workspace tests, 0 failed, 1 ignored, all 7 gates green.

The Phase 4.9 trifecta is R1C1 reference syntax + locales (en/de/fr) +
the `@` implicit-intersection operator. Per design § 4.4 the on-disk +
op-log canonical form is ALWAYS A1+EnUs; the workbook's reference_mode
+ locale settings are display-only preferences.

## Commits in scope (chronological)

```

                                RangeRef::R1C1Cells)

                                no ExprPlan variant)
```

## Areas to probe (in priority order)

### A. Cross-sub-phase invariants

These are the "only surfaces when N≥2 sub-phases meet" issues.

1. **Canonical-storage round-trip determinism (design § 4.4
   contract).** Save a workbook with R1C1 mode + DE locale and a
   formula stored canonically. Reload. Now `set_formula` again with
   text that should canonicalize to the same A1+EnUs form. Does the
   PlanCache hit, OR does the loaded workbook's separate process
   start fresh and re-bind? If start-fresh, no problem; if there's
   any shared cache state, check for staleness.

2. **`@` AST → canonical text round-trip.** Walk through:
   `parse("@Sheet1!A1")` → AST with `ImplicitIntersection(CellRef
   { sheet: Name("Sheet1"), col: 0, row: 0 })` → `print_with(.., A1,
   EnUs, None)` → `@Sheet1!A1` or `@Sheet1!A1` ? The W5-143
   printer emits `@<inner>` where inner's emission includes the
   sheet prefix. Confirm the AST round-trip test pinned at
   `printer::tests::sheet_at_cellref_round_trips_via_ast` is
   correct AND that no production code path produces the alternate
   `Sheet1!@A1` shape (it's accepted on PARSE but never emitted
   on PRINT). Verify no test pin asserts the wrong order.

3. **Op-log replay × canonical-storage interaction.** Producer
   writes `R[-1]C` in R1C1 mode at cell (1, 0). Canonical storage
   stores `A1`. Op-log emits `PutFormula { text: "A1" }`. Replay
   into a fresh workbook (still default A1 mode) re-applies the
   canonical text. Does the replay's `lex_with(A1, EnUs)` correctly
   re-bind? AND if the producer also emitted `Op::SetReferenceMode
   { R1C1 }` BEFORE the formula, does that change anything? (Per
   design canon: it shouldn't — the stored text is canonical
   regardless of mode.)

4. **Implicit intersection × range × site interaction.** The 4.9.H
   binder narrows `@A1:A10` at bind time using `site.cell`. The
   set_formula path passes `BindSite::at_cell(addr)`. But the
   PlanCache key is `(canonical_text, sheet, name_gen)` — NOT cell-
   specific. If the SAME canonical text `@A1:A10` is bound at cell
   (5, 0) → cached as `CellRef(A6)`, then bound at cell (10, 0) →
   cache HIT returns `CellRef(A6)` (WRONG — should be A11). Check
   whether this collision exists and whether the cache key needs
   to include cell coords for `@`-bearing formulas.

5. **Workbook-scope mode/locale change × stored formulas.** Per the
   W5-148 `reference_mode_change_leaves_stored_formula_text_canonical`
   test, the stored text stays canonical when the user switches
   modes. But: does the PlanCache get invalidated? If a formula
   was bound at cache miss, the plan is stored. The next time
   set_formula sees the same canonical text, it cache-hits with the
   pre-mode-change plan. That's actually fine (the plan doesn't
   depend on mode). But: confirm.

### B. R1C1 forms — parser/printer/binder triangle

6. **AxisSpec arithmetic correctness.** `R[-1]C[2]` at anchor
   (5, 5) → ExprPlan::CellRef { row: 4, col: 7 }. Anchor at (0, 0)
   with R[-1] → resolved row would be -1. The W5-140 binder uses
   i64 to detect underflow → `BindError::R1C1OutOfBounds`. Verify
   the i64 arithmetic doesn't have a sign-extension bug for
   AxisSpec::Rel(i32::MIN).

7. **Mixed-relativity range rejection coverage.** `build_range`
   checks `axis_relativity_matches(a_row, b_row)` AND
   `axis_relativity_matches(a_col, b_col)`. Per-axis. What about
   `R1C[1]:R10C[5]` — both rows absolute, both cols relative.
   Should that be accepted? My read of design § 3.1 says yes (each
   axis independently consistent). Confirm the existing parser
   tests cover this case and not just "all-or-nothing relativity."

8. **R1C1 absolute axis bound = `MAX_ROW + 1` / `MAX_COLUMN + 1`.**
   The lexer accepts `R1048576C16384` (= MAX_ROW+1, MAX_COLUMN+1)
   and emits AxisSpec::Abs(1048576) / Abs(16384). The binder maps
   `Abs(n) → n - 1` (0-indexed). `1048576 - 1 = MAX_ROW`. OK. But:
   the lexer rejects `R1048577` as `RowTooLarge`. Verify the
   bound is exactly `MAX_ROW + 1` (1048576) on the lexer side, not
   off-by-one.

### C. Locale handling — printer / loader / op-log triangle

9. **Locale `Unknown` propagation.** The W5-145 qbook v7 loader
   captures unknown locale strings to `LocaleWire::Unknown(s)` and
   surfaces `QbookError::UnknownLocale { found }`. The W5-146
   op-log replay has the parallel path: `LocaleWire::Unknown(s)` →
   `ReplayError::UnknownLocale { index, found }`. But: is there
   any production code path where an unknown locale could be
   SAVED? (The save-side `from_runtime` only emits canonical
   variants.) If yes, we have a save-side bug. If no, we're fine.

10. **Locale × number printing precision.** The W5-142 printer
    swaps `.` → locale glyph in Rust's f64 Display output. What
    about numbers in scientific notation? `1e-10` prints as
    `0.0000000001` (Rust f64 Display) — has a single `.`. In DE,
    the swap produces `0,0000000001`. Confirmed by the
    `print_with_de_locale_small_fraction` test. But what about
    `format!("{n}")` producing an actual `e` notation (very large
    or very small magnitudes)? Verify the swap doesn't accidentally
    convert `1e10` → `1,10` or similar.

11. **DE/FR symmetry.** Locale `Fr` is documented as identical to
    `De` per design § 3.2. The W5-142 tests have one FR-specific
    test but most are DE. Spot-check that FR doesn't diverge in
    any helper or table.

### D. Implicit intersection — design § 3.3 rule coverage

12. **Rule 7 (array result) — what's the current behavior?**
    W5-144 deferred this to a "known gap" — array-returning
    functions under `@` return `#CALC!` per the pre-existing
    4.7.G limit. The user-visible impact: `@TRANSPOSE(A1:A10)`
    returns `#CALC!` instead of A1 (top-left). Should this be
    a HIGH (since TRANSPOSE+@ is a documented Excel pattern) or
    MEDIUM (rare in practice)?

13. **Rule 9 (StructuredRef inner) — is_this_row patch
    correctness.** W5-144 patches `is_this_row: true` on the inner
    StructuredRef result. Confirm this doesn't break the
    `Sales[@Col]` case (which already has `is_this_row: true`
    from the parser). Specifically: parse `Sales[@Col]` → inner
    spec is `ThisRowColumn("Col")` → resolve_structured_ref
    returns `is_this_row: true`. Wrapping in `@` and re-patching
    is idempotent — but confirm the binder test covers this.

14. **Rule 8 (function inner) array-return scalar-context
    interaction.** `@SUM(A1:A10)` where A1:A10 has 10 values →
    SUM returns scalar 55 (no spill). Works. But `@TRANSPOSE(A1:A10)`
    → TRANSPOSE returns array → scalar context returns `#CALC!`.
    Documented above (rule 7). NO additional concern beyond #12.

### E. Persistence × op-log

15. **v7 persistence × op-log SetReferenceMode/SetLocale
    interaction.** Save a workbook in R1C1 + DE state. The v7
    envelope writes `reference_mode = "R1C1"`, `locale = "de"`.
    The op-log file contains `Op::SetReferenceMode { R1C1 }` and
    `Op::SetLocale { De }` from the runtime-API calls. On load,
    the envelope sets R1C1+DE. The replay then RE-applies
    SetReferenceMode + SetLocale (idempotent — no-op since the
    state already matches). OK. But: what if the envelope was
    hand-edited to A1+EnUs but the op-log still has the SetReferenceMode
    op? The replay would CHANGE the workbook to R1C1+DE after
    load. Is that intended? (Probably yes — the op-log is the
    source of truth for the post-load state.) Confirm.

16. **v6 → v7 migration path.** A user with a v6 .qbook saves
    on the new build. Save writes v7 (with reference_mode + locale
    fields omitted since default). Load that v7. Then save again.
    Diff the two v7 saves — byte-identical? The forward-compat
    assertion in the loader only fires for `schema_version < 7`
    AND v7-field-present. The new save path always emits v7 with
    fields omitted for defaults. Confirm round-trip determinism.

### F. Calcgraph

17. **`@` fingerprint stability.** W5-143 added fingerprint tag
    byte 13 for `Expr::ImplicitIntersection`. Confirm this byte
    doesn't collide with any other variant (the tags are 0-12 +
    13 for ImplicitIntersection). Pre-existing tags: Number=0,
    String=1, Bool=2, CellRef=3, RangeRef=4, Binary=5, Unary=6,
    Function=7, Array=8, Spill=9, NameRef=10, Error=11,
    StructuredRef=12. So 13 is correct.

18. **R1C1Ref / R1C1Cells `unreachable!()` arms — never reached
    in practice?** All 5 calcgraph sites (fingerprint × 2, graph,
    stripes × 2) panic on these variants. The contract is "storage
    canon lowers before calcgraph." Confirm the canonicalization
    in `set_formula` produces canonical A1 ALWAYS, so the
    fingerprinter / stripe index never sees the intermediate
    forms. NO test today directly verifies this; the closing
    audit might want one (an integration test that puts an R1C1
    AST through the binder and confirms no `Expr::R1C1Ref`
    survives in the bound `ExprPlan`).

### G. Test coverage gaps

19. List any obvious gaps. Specifically: every public method
    added in W5-138 → W5-149 — is there at least one direct test?
    At least one round-trip test? Test count went 2443 → 2611
    (+168). For the load-bearing surfaces (lex_with mode/locale,
    print_with mode/locale/site, set_reference_mode, set_locale,
    set_formula canon flow), confirm each has direct + integration
    coverage.

### H. Documentation drift

20. Walk through the design doc § 5 sub-phase table. Every row
    should have a "shipped" annotation citing the W5-XXX commit
    that landed it. The doc was updated per-commit; spot-check
    that no row is stale or missing a citation.

## Output

Triage findings as:

- **HIGH** = ship blocker; the engine can produce an inconsistent
  state or a crash from normal use.
- **MEDIUM** = correctness or robustness issue; user-visible but
  recoverable.
- **LOW** = polish; doc drift, dead code, naming, etc.

Each finding should cite: file path, line range, one-sentence
description of what's wrong, one-sentence proposed fix.

     1	//! Phase 2B.3 (2026-05-12) — bind-plan cache V0.
     2	//!
     3	//! Caches parsed + bound [`ExprPlan`] values keyed by the formula text, the
     4	//! owning sheet, and the workbook's NameTable generation. Lives on
     5	//! [`crate::WorkbookRuntime`] and is shared into spawned
     6	//! [`crate::WorkbookTransaction`] instances through forwarding.
     7	//!
     8	//! ## Invalidation contract
     9	//!
    10	//! Cache entries are NOT explicitly removed on workbook mutation. Instead,
    11	//! the cache key is `(formula_text, sheet_id, name_table_generation)`. When
    12	//! the workbook mutates state that would change a bind result:
    13	//!
    14	//! - User edits formula text → new text, new key, automatic miss.
    15	//! - Caller registers/removes a name → `NameTable::generation()` advances
    16	//!   (per Phase 2B.3 workbook.rs), so plans bound against the prior
    17	//!   generation no longer match.
    18	//!
    19	//! Stale entries accumulate over time; Phase 3 calcgraph integration adds
    20	//! explicit eviction tied to dirty propagation. For Phase 2B.3 the cache
    21	//! is small enough that drift is acceptable — typical workbook formula
    22	//! cardinality is in the thousands, not millions.
    23	//!
    24	//! ## What the cache stores
    25	//!
    26	//! `Arc<ExprPlan>` so cache hits hand back a refcount-bump without cloning
    27	//! the plan tree. `ExprPlan` is `Clone + Debug + PartialEq` per
    28	//! `ql-exec::plan` but the plan tree is non-trivial; sharing via `Arc`
    29	//! keeps the cache cheap.
    30	//!
    31	//! ## References
    32	//!
    33	//! - `.references/hyperformula/src/parser/ParserWithCaching.ts` — HF caches
    34	//!   parsed ASTs keyed by formula text + sheet metadata for the same
    35	//!   reason. They evict on text change; we additionally include
    36	//!   `NameTable::generation` so the bind result (not just the parse) stays
    37	//!   consistent.
    38	//! - `.references/hyperformula/src/parser/Cache.ts` — the size-bounded LRU
    39	//!   wrapper. V0 here is unbounded; a bounded eviction policy lands when
    40	//!   workbook size makes it necessary.
    41	
    42	use std::collections::HashMap;
    43	use std::sync::Arc;
    44	
    45	use ql_types::SheetId;
    46	
    47	use crate::plan::ExprPlan;
    48	
    49	/// Composite cache key. Reflects everything that, if changed, would change
    50	/// the bind result for a given formula text:
    51	///
    52	/// - `text` — the raw formula source (without leading `=`). Different text
    53	///   yields a different parse tree, so plans don't share.
    54	/// - `sheet` — owning sheet for the binding. Same text in different sheets
    55	///   binds to different cell references (relative refs resolve against the
    56	///   owning sheet).
    57	/// - `name_gen` — `NameTable::generation()` at the moment of bind. A
    58	///   subsequent name registration / removal bumps this, so old plans
    59	///   become unreachable.
    60	#[derive(Clone, Debug, Hash, Eq, PartialEq)]
    61	pub struct PlanCacheKey {
    62	    pub text: Arc<str>,
    63	    pub sheet: SheetId,
    64	    pub name_gen: u64,
    65	}

    67	/// Snapshot of cache observability data — exposed via

    70	pub struct PlanCacheStats {

    75	
    76	impl PlanCacheStats {
    77	    /// Hit rate as a fraction in `[0.0, 1.0]`. Returns `None` when no
    78	    /// lookups have happened yet so callers don't divide by zero or
    79	    /// display "0.0%" for an unused cache.
    80	    pub fn hit_rate(&self) -> Option<f64> {
    81	        let total = self.hits + self.misses;
    82	        if total == 0 {
    83	            None

    85	            Some(self.hits as f64 / total as f64)

codex
I found one concrete integration blocker so far: bind-time `@` range narrowing produces cell-specific plans, but the cache key is not cell-specific. I’m doing one quick pass over the persistence/op-log and locale snippets to avoid inventing secondary findings.

codex
## Findings

### HIGH-1: PlanCache reuses cell-specific `@` range plans
File: crates/ql-exec/src/workbook_runtime.rs:592-610
Issue: `set_formula` caches by canonical text + sheet + name generation while binding with `BindSite::at_cell(addr)`, so repeated formulas like `@A:A`, `@1:1`, or `@A1:A10` in different cells can reuse a plan narrowed for the first cell.
Fix: Include formula row/col in the cache key for cell-dependent binds, or bypass/segregate the cache for `@` formulas that narrow ranges at bind time.

## Verdict

NOT SHIP-READY

Phase 4.9 has a ship-blocking canonical-storage/cache integration bug: `@` is preserved in canonical text, but some `@` plans are intentionally bound to a specific formula cell, and the cache key does not encode that cell. That can produce wrong evaluated values from normal repeated-formula use despite green tests.

## Findings

### HIGH-1: PlanCache reuses cell-specific `@` range plans
File: crates/ql-exec/src/workbook_runtime.rs:592-610
Issue: `set_formula` caches by canonical text + sheet + name generation while binding with `BindSite::at_cell(addr)`, so repeated formulas like `@A:A`, `@1:1`, or `@A1:A10` in different cells can reuse a plan narrowed for the first cell.
Fix: Include formula row/col in the cache key for cell-dependent binds, or bypass/segregate the cache for `@` formulas that narrow ranges at bind time.

## Verdict

NOT SHIP-READY

Phase 4.9 has a ship-blocking canonical-storage/cache integration bug: `@` is preserved in canonical text, but some `@` plans are intentionally bound to a specific formula cell, and the cache key does not encode that cell. That can produce wrong evaluated values from normal repeated-formula use despite green tests.
